Mariusz Kaniecki and Justyna Kosakowska
1

Abstract




m

Two partial orders on Standard Young Tableaux with applications to invariant subspaces of nilpotent linear operators

1
Introduction

The main aim of this paper is to generalize results of
[
13
,
14
,
16
]
, where applications of two partial orders on
standard Young tableaux (and Littlewood-Richardson tableaux)
in the theory of invariant subspaces are presented.

Let
k
be a field and let
\Lambda=k[p]
be the algebra of polynomials with coefficients in
k
and one variable
p
. Nilpotent finite dimensional
\Lambda
-modules we call
nilpotent linear operators
. Let
\mathcal{S}=\mathcal{S}(\Lambda)
be the category of embeddings
(A\subset B)
, where
A,B
are nilpotent nilpotent linear operators. Objects of
\mathcal{S}
we call
invariant subspaces of nilpotent linear operators
(shortly
invariant subspaces
or
embeddings
), see Section
4
for precise definitions.

Let
\alpha
be a partition of a given weight
|\alpha|=r
and let
\mathcal{T}_{\alpha}
be the set of all standard Young tableaux of shape
\alpha
, see Section
2
for definitions. In
[
16
]
two partial orders
\leq_{\rm box}
and
\leq_{\rm dom}
on
\mathcal{T}_{\alpha}
are investigated. There are also shown connections of these orders with partial orders defined for some classes of invariant subspaces of nilpotent linear operators, see
[
16
,
14
]
.

In the present paper we extend definitions of the orders
\leq_{\rm box}
and
\leq_{\rm dom}
to the set

\mathcal{T}_{r}=\bigcup_{\alpha;|\alpha|=r}\mathcal{T}_{\alpha}.

Moreover in Section
5
we define partial orders
\leq_{\rm ext}
and
\leq_{\rm hom}
for suitably chosen subcategory of
\mathcal{S}(\Lambda)
. Applying Littlewood-Richardson tableaux and their connections with objects of
\mathcal{S}(\Lambda)
(see Sections
3
,
4.1
), we connect orders
\leq_{\rm ext}
and
\leq_{\rm hom}
with properties of
\mathcal{T}_{r}
. One of the main aims of the paper is to prove the following theorem.

Theorem 1.1
.

For any integer
r\geq 1
, we have

\leq_{\rm box}=\leq_{\rm dom}

on
\mathcal{T}_{r}
.

1.1
Motivations and related results

Partial orders
\leq_{\rm box}=\leq_{\rm dom}
we consider on
\mathcal{T}_{r}
contains all orders presented in
[
26
]
.

The problem of classifying objects of
\mathcal{S}(\Lambda)
, up to isomorphism,
has its origin in
[
3
]
, where Birkhoff proposes to
classify all embeddings of a subgroup in an
abelian group, up to automorphisms of the ambient group.
There are many versions of the problem: one can take for the coefficient ring
any local uniserial ring or one can admit several submodules. It is known that in general this problem is "wild",
i.e. there is no chance to get nice description of all objects
up to isomorphism, see
[
1
,
24
]
. However, there are many results developing
this theory, for example
[
8
,
2
,
23
,
24
,
25
,
6
,
7
,
18
,
17
]
. In particular, the papers
[
25
,
18
,
17
]
discuss important interrelations between the invariant subspaces and the categories
{\rm coh}\mathbb{X}
of coherent sheaves over weighted projective lines
{\rm coh}\mathbb{X}
and singularity theory.
Recently, in
[
7
]
, the authors
applied derived categories of coherent
sheaves
\mathcal{D}^{b}({\rm coh}\mathbb{X})
to obtain a description of some objects of the category of invariant
subspaces of nilpotent linear operators.
Nilpotent operators and their invariant subspaces appear in a natural way in representation theory

[
17
,
18
,
21
,
24
,
25
]
. They are also special cases of monic representations
[
19
,
27
,
28
]
.

1.2
Organization of the paper

In Section
2
we recall notation and definitions connected with standard Young tableaux, recall definition of
\leq_{\rm box}
,
\leq_{\rm dom}
on
\mathcal{T}_{\alpha}
and extend them to the partial orders on
\mathcal{T}_{r}
. Moreover we prove that
\leq_{\rm dom}\subseteq\leq_{\rm box}
.

In Section
3
we recall definition of Littlewood-Richardson tableaux (shortly LR-tableaux) of shape
\beta\setminus\gamma
and content
\alpha
. In the case
\beta\setminus\gamma
is a rook strip we define
\leq_{\rm box}
,
\leq_{\rm dom}
on the set
\mathcal{T}_{r,\gamma}^{\beta}
of LR-tableaux of shape
\beta\setminus\gamma
and content
\alpha
with
|\alpha|=r
. We prove that
\leq_{\rm box}
,
\leq_{\rm dom}
are invariant under the bijection
3.1

\Phi^{\beta}_{\gamma}:\mathcal{T}_{r,\gamma}^{\beta}\to\mathcal{T}_{r}.

In Section
4
we give notation and definitions connected with the category
\mathcal{S}(\Lambda)
, show connections of objects of
\mathcal{S}(\Lambda)
with LR-tableaux and define two types of objects
\mathcal{S}(\Lambda)
.

In Section
5
we define algebraic partial orders
\leq_{\rm ext}
and
\leq_{\rm hom}
and prove that
\leq_{\rm ext}\subseteq\leq_{\rm hom}\subseteq\leq_{\rm dom}
.

Finally, in Section
6
we prove that
\leq_{\rm box}\subseteq\leq_{\rm ext}
by constructing to any box-move a suitable short exact sequence of objects of
\mathcal{S}(\Lambda)
. This finishes proof of Theorem
1.1
.

2
Two partial orders on SYTs

In this section we generalize the main result of
[
16
]
.

2.1
Partitions and standard Young tableaux

A
partition

\alpha=(\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{s})
is a finite, weakly decreasing sequence of natural numbers. The number
s
is called the
length
of the partition
\alpha
, and sum
|\alpha|=\alpha_{1}+\ldots+\alpha_{s}
is called its
weight
. Given natural number
r
, we denote by
\mathcal{P}_{r}
the set of all partitions of weight
r
and by
\mathcal{P}
the set of all partitions.

A partition
\alpha
we visually represent by its
Young diagram
, consisting of top-aligned
s
columns composed of
\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{s}
cells, respectively. Since there is an obvious bijection between the set of all Young diagrams and the set of all partitions, the Young diagram connected with a partition
\alpha
is also denoted by
\alpha
.
By
\overline{\alpha}=(\overline{\alpha}_{1},\ldots,\overline{\alpha}_{\alpha_{1}})
we denote the partition
transposed
to a partition
\alpha
, i.e. the Young diagram of
\overline{\alpha}
is the transposition of the Young diagram
\alpha
:

\overline{\alpha}_{j}=\#\{i\,:\;\alpha_{i}\geq j\}.

The picture below shows an example of two Young diagrams associated with partitions
\alpha=(3,2,2,1)
and
\overline{\alpha}=(4,3,1)
, respectively.

\alpha\,:

\overline{\alpha}\,:

Remark:

In all diagrams we number rows starting from top and going down.

We say that partitions
\alpha,\beta\in\mathcal{P}_{r}
of the same weight
r
are in
the natural order

\alpha\leq_{\rm nat}\beta

if for any
c
there is

\sum\limits_{i=0}^{c}\alpha_{i}\geq\sum\limits_{i=0}^{c}\beta_{i}

or equivalently

\sum\limits_{i=0}^{c}\overline{\alpha}_{i}\leq\sum\limits_{i=0}^{c}\overline{\beta}_{i},

see

[
20
, 1.9]
. It is straightforward to check that
\leq_{\rm nat}
is a partial order on
\mathcal{P}_{r}
.

We define a new relation on
\mathcal{P}
(also denoted by
\leq_{\rm nat}
). Assume that
\alpha,\beta\in\mathcal{P}
are arbitrary partitions (not necessarily of the same weight!). We write
\alpha\leq_{\rm nat}\beta

if for any
c
there is

\sum\limits_{i=0}^{c}\overline{\alpha}_{i}\leq\sum\limits_{i=0}^{c}\overline{\beta}_{i}
. Note that if
|\alpha|\neq|\beta|
it might be not equivalent to
\sum\limits_{i=0}^{c}\alpha_{i}\geq\sum\limits_{i=0}^{c}\beta_{i}
for any
c
(for example
\alpha=(1),\beta=(2)
). The both orders are coincides on
\mathcal{P}_{\alpha}
for any partition
\alpha
.

It is easy to see that
\leq_{\rm nat}
is reflexive and transitive on
\mathcal{P}
. To see that it is a partial order, it is enough to observe that for partition
\alpha,\beta
(not necessarily of the same length) with
\alpha\leq_{\rm nat}\beta
and
\beta\leq_{\rm nat}\alpha
we have
|\alpha|=|\beta|
. In this case
\alpha,\beta\in\mathcal{P}_{|\alpha|}
and
\alpha=\beta
, because
\leq_{\rm nat}
is a partial order on
\mathcal{P}_{|\alpha|}
. It follows that
\leq_{\rm nat}
is a partial order on
\mathcal{P}
and we call it
the natural order
.

Assume that
\gamma\subseteq\beta
. The
skew diagram

\beta\setminus\gamma
is a diagram obtained by visual extraction of the Young diagram
\gamma
from the Young diagram
\beta
when upper left corners of both are in the same place. The skew diagram
\beta\setminus\gamma
is said to be a
horizontal strip
if
\beta_{i}\leq\gamma_{i}+1
holds for each
i=1,\ldots,s
and a
vertical strip
if
\overline{\beta}\setminus\overline{\gamma}
is a horizontal strip. A
rook strip
is both vertical and horizontal strip.

The picture below shows an example of the horizontal strip
\beta\setminus\gamma
and the vertical strip
\overline{\beta}\setminus\overline{\gamma}
, where
\beta=(3,2,2,1)
and
\gamma=(2,1,1)
.

\beta\setminus\gamma\,:

\overline{\beta}\setminus\overline{\gamma}\,:

Definition:

Let
\alpha
be a partition. A
standard Young tableau
of shape
\alpha
(SYT(
\alpha
) for brevity) is the Young diagram
\alpha
filled with natural numbers
1,\ldots,|\alpha|
that strictly increase along rows and down columns. Denote by
\mathcal{T}_{\alpha}

the set of all SYT(
\alpha
).

Remark:

A SYT(
\alpha
)
\Sigma
can be identified with the
sequence

\Sigma=[\sigma^{(1)},\ldots,\sigma^{(|\alpha|)}]
of partitions such that

\sigma^{(1)}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\sigma^{(|\alpha|)}
, where
the skew diagram

\sigma^{(e)}\setminus\sigma^{(e-1)}
carries the entry
for all
e=1,\ldots,|\alpha|
. We set
\sigma^{(0)}=(0)
.

Example:

Let
\alpha=(3,2,2)
be a partition and
\Sigma
be SYT
(\alpha)
of the form

\Sigma=\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\vtop{\halign{&\opttoksa@YT={\font@YT}\getcolor@YT{\save@YT{\opttoksb@YT}}\nil@YT\getcolor@YT{\startbox@@YT\the\opttoksa@YT\the\opttoksb@YT}#\endbox@YT\cr\lower The tableau
\Sigma
can be be identified with the
sequence

\Sigma=[\sigma^{(1)},\ldots,\sigma^{(7)}],

where
\sigma^{(1)}=(1),\;\sigma^{(2)}=(1,1),\;\sigma^{(3)}=(2,1),\;\sigma^{(4)}=(2,1,1),\;\sigma^{(5)}=(3,1,1),\;\sigma^{(6)}=(3,2,1),\;\sigma^{(7)}=(3,2,2).

Given
r\in\mathbb{N}
we use the notation

\mathcal{T}_{r}=\bigcup_{\alpha;|\alpha|=r}\mathcal{T}_{\alpha}.

2.2
Two partial orders on
\mathcal{T}_{r}

Definition:

1.

Let
\Sigma=[\sigma^{(1)},\ldots,\sigma^{(r)}],=[\pi^{(1)},\ldots,\pi^{(r)}]\in\mathcal{T}_{r}
. We say that
\Pi\leq_{\rm dom}\Sigma
in
the dominance order
if

\pi^{(i)}\leq_{\rm nat}\sigma^{(i)}
for all
i=1,\ldots,r
.
Note
that we do not require that
\Sigma
and
\Pi
have the same shape.

2.

Suppose
\Sigma,\Pi\in\mathcal{T}_{r}
.
We say
\Pi
is obtained from
\Sigma
by
a decreasing box move
if

\Pi
is obtained by swapping two entries in
\Sigma
so that the smaller entry
winds up in the higher-numbered row (in this case the SYTs
\Sigma,\Pi
have the same shape); or if

\Pi
is obtained by winding up the last box of a row of
\Sigma
to the last position of a higher-numbered row (in this case the SYTs
\Sigma,\Pi
have different shapes).
We denote by
\leq_{\sf box}
the partial order generated by all box moves.

Remark:

In
[
16
]
the partial orders
\leq_{\rm dom}
and
\leq_{\rm box}
were defined and investigated on the set

\mathcal{T}_{\alpha}
for a given partition
\alpha
.

Example:

Here is an example, let

\Pi=\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\vtop{\halign{&\opttoksa@YT={\font@YT}\getcolor@YT{\save@YT{\opttoksb@YT}}\nil@YT\getcolor@YT{\startbox@@YT\the\opttoksa@YT\the\opttoksb@YT}#\endbox@YT\cr\lower The tableaux
\Sigma,\Pi
can be be identified with the
sequences

\Pi=[\pi^{(1)},\ldots,\pi^{(5)}],\Sigma=[\sigma^{(1)},\ldots,\sigma^{(5)}],

where
\pi^{(1)}=(1),\;\pi^{(2)}=(2),\;\pi^{(3)}=(2,1),\;\pi^{(4)}=(3,1),\;\pi^{(5)}=(3,2)
and

\sigma^{(1)}=(1),\;\sigma^{(2)}=(1,1),\;\sigma^{(3)}=(1,1,1),\;\sigma^{(4)}=(2,1,1),\;\sigma^{(5)}=(2,2,1).

One can check that
\pi^{(i)}\leq_{\rm nat}\sigma^{(i)}
for all
i
, so
\Pi\leq_{\rm dom}\Sigma
. Moreover we have following sequence of decreasing box moves transforming tableau
\Sigma
into tableau
\Pi
:

0.39993pt\vbox to8.4pt{\vss\hbox to8.00006pt{\hss$\scriptstyle 5$\hss}\vss}\kern-8.4pt\vrule height=8.4pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 8.00006pt\vrule height=8.4pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-8.4pt\hrule width=8.79993pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 8.00006pt\hrule width=8.79993pt,height=0.39993pt}\cr}}\kern 690.4pt}\kern 690.4pt}}$}<_{\rm box}\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\vtop{\halign{&\opttoksa@YT={\font@YT}\getcolor@YT{\save@YT{\opttoksb@YT}}\nil@YT\getcolor@YT{\startbox@@YT\the\opttoksa@YT\the\opttoksb@YT}#\endbox@YT\cr\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to8.4pt{\vss\hbox to8.00006pt{\hss$\scriptstyle$\hss}\vss}\kern-8.4pt\vrule height=8.4pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 8.00006pt\vrule height=8.4pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-8.4pt\hrule width=8.79993pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 8.00006pt\hrule width=8.79993pt,height=0.39993pt}&\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to8.4pt{\vss\hbox to8.00006pt{\hss$\scriptstyle$\hss}\vss}\kern-8.4pt\vrule height=8.4pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 8.00006pt\vrule height=8.4pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-8.4pt\hrule width=8.79993pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 8.00006pt\hrule width=8.79993pt,height=0.39993pt}&\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to8.4pt{\vss\hbox to8.00006pt{\hss$\scriptstyle$\hss}\vss}\kern-8.4pt\vrule height=8.4pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 8.00006pt\vrule height=8.4pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-8.4pt\hrule width=8.79993pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 8.00006pt\hrule width=8.79993pt,height=0.39993pt}\cr\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to8.4pt{\vss\hbox to8.00006pt{\hss$\scriptstyle$\hss}\vss}\kern-8.4pt\vrule height=8.4pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 8.00006pt\vrule height=8.4pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-8.4pt\hrule width=8.79993pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 8.00006pt\hrule width=8.79993pt,height=0.39993pt}&\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to8.4pt{\vss\hbox to8.00006pt{\hss$\scriptstyle$\hss}\vss}\kern-8.4pt\vrule height=8.4pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 8.00006pt\vrule height=8.4pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-8.4pt\hrule width=8.79993pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 8.00006pt\hrule width=8.79993pt,height=0.39993pt}\cr}}\hbox{\vtop{\halign{&\opttoksa@YT={\font@YT}\getcolor@YT{\save@YT{\opttoksb@YT}}\nil@YT\getcolor@YT{\startbox@@YT\the\opttoksa@YT\the\opttoksb@YT}#\endbox@YT\cr\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to8.4pt{\vss\hbox to8.00006pt{\hss$\scriptstyle 1$\hss}\vss}\kern-8.4pt\vrule height=8.4pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 8.00006pt\vrule height=8.4pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-8.4pt\hrule width=8.79993pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 8.00006pt\hrule width=8.79993pt,height=0.39993pt}&\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to8.4pt{\vss\hbox to8.00006pt{\hss$\scriptstyle 2$\hss}\vss}\kern-8.4pt\vrule height=8.4pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 8.00006pt\vrule height=8.4pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-8.4pt\hrule width=8.79993pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 8.00006pt\hrule width=8.79993pt,height=0.39993pt}&\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to8.4pt{\vss\hbox to8.00006pt{\hss$\scriptstyle 3$\hss}\vss}\kern-8.4pt\vrule height=8.4pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 8.00006pt\vrule height=8.4pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-8.4pt\hrule width=8.79993pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 8.00006pt\hrule width=8.79993pt,height=0.39993pt}\cr\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to8.4pt{\vss\hbox to8.00006pt{\hss$\scriptstyle 4$\hss}\vss}\kern-8.4pt\vrule height=8.4pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 8.00006pt\vrule height=8.4pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-8.4pt\hrule width=8.79993pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 8.00006pt\hrule width=8.79993pt,height=0.39993pt}&\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to8.4pt{\vss\hbox to8.00006pt{\hss$\scriptstyle 5$\hss}\vss}\kern-8.4pt\vrule height=8.4pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 8.00006pt\vrule height=8.4pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-8.4pt\hrule width=8.79993pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 8.00006pt\hrule width=8.79993pt,height=0.39993pt}\cr}}\kern 690.4pt}\kern 690.4pt}}$}=\Sigma,

First in
\Sigma
we swap entries
3
and
4
, then we wind box with entry
4
from the first to the third row and finally (to get
\Pi
) we swap entries
2
and
3
. It follows that
\Pi\leq_{\rm box}\Sigma
.

We are going to prove that
\leq_{\rm dom}
implies
\leq_{\rm box}
. Let
r\in\mathbb{N}
and let
\underline{r}
denotes the partition

\underline{r}=(r,r,\ldots,r)
of length

r
with all entries equal to
r
. We define a function

f:\mathcal{T}_{r}\to\mathcal{T}_{\underline{r}}

(2.1)

in the following way.

Let
\Pi=[\pi^{(1)},\ldots,\pi^{(r)}]\in\mathcal{T}_{r}
. For any
s=0,\ldots,r^{2}-r-1
we define recursively
\pi^{(r+s)}
:

\overline{\pi^{(r+s+1)}_{i}}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\overline{\pi^{(r+s)}_{i}}&\mbox{ for }i\neq j_{s}+1\\
\overline{\pi^{(r+s)}_{i}}+1&\mbox{ for }i=j_{s}+1\end{array}\right.

(2.2)

where
j_{s}
is equal to the number of rows of length
r
in Young diagram of
\pi^{(r+s)}
. Formally by
j_{s}=j_{s}^{\Pi}\in\{1,\ldots,r-1\}
we denote the maximal number with the property

\overline{\pi^{(r+s)}_{j_{s}}}=r

(2.3)

or if there is no number with the property, we set
j_{s}=0
.

It follows that, for all
s
,
\overline{\pi^{(r+s+1)}}
is a partition.
We set

f(\Pi)=[\pi^{(1)},\ldots,\pi^{(r)},\pi^{(r+1)},\ldots,\pi^{(r^{2})}].

(2.4)

It is easy to see that
f(\Pi)\in\mathcal{T}_{\underline{r}}
.

Lemma 2.5
.

Let
\Sigma=[\sigma^{(1)},\ldots,\sigma^{(r)}],\Pi=[\pi^{(1)},\ldots,\pi^{(r)}]\in\mathcal{T}_{r}
. Then

1.

\Pi\leq_{\rm dom}\Sigma
if and only if
f(\Pi)\leq_{\rm dom}f(\Sigma)
,

2.

if
f(\Pi)\leq_{\rm box}f(\Sigma)
, then
\Pi\leq_{\rm box}\Sigma
,

3.

if
\Pi\leq_{\rm dom}\Sigma
, then
\Pi\leq_{\rm box}\Sigma
.

Proof.

1. Assume that
\Pi\leq_{\rm dom}\Sigma
. It follows that
\pi^{(i)}\leq_{\rm nat}\sigma^{(i)}
for all
i=1,\ldots,r
. By the induction on
s
we prove that
\pi^{(r+s+1)}\leq_{\rm nat}\sigma^{(r+s+1)}
for all
s=-1,0,\ldots,r^{2}-r-1
. For
s=-1
, the condition is obvious. Let
s\geq 0
and let
l_{s}=j_{s}^{\Sigma},j_{s}=j_{s}^{\Pi}
be as above.

Note that
j_{s}\leq l_{s}
. Indeed, if
j_{s}=0
, we are done. Otherwise, by the choice of
j_{s}
we have

\overline{\pi_{j_{s}+1}^{(r+s)}}<r
and

\overline{\pi_{i}^{(r+s+1)}}=\overline{\pi_{i}^{(r+s)}}=r
for
1\leq i\leq j_{s}
. Therefore by the induction we get:

j_{s}\cdot r=\sum_{i=1}^{j_{s}}\overline{\pi_{i}^{(r+s)}}\leq\sum_{i=1}^{j_{s}}\overline{\sigma_{i}^{(r+s)}}\leq j_{s}\cdot r,

(2.6)

because all parts of all partitions we consider are less or equal
r
. It follows from (
2.6
) that

\overline{\sigma}_{i}^{(r+s)}=r
for all
i=1,\ldots,j_{s}
. By the choice of
l_{s}
, we get
l_{s}\geq j_{s}
.

Note that for all
t
we have

\sum_{i=1}^{t}\overline{\sigma^{(r+s+1)}_{i}}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\overline{\sigma^{(r+s)}_{i}}&\mbox{ for }i\leq l_{s}\\
\sum_{i=1}^{t}\overline{\sigma^{(r+s)}_{i}}+1&\mbox{ for }i\geq l_{s}+1\end{array}\right.

(2.7)

and

\sum_{i=1}^{t}\overline{\pi^{(r+s+1)}_{i}}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\overline{\pi^{(r+s)}_{i}}&\mbox{ for }i\leq j_{s}\\
\sum_{i=1}^{t}\overline{\pi^{(r+s)}_{i}}+1&\mbox{ for }i\geq j_{s}+1\end{array}\right.

(2.8)

For
t\leq j_{s}
and
t>l_{s}
, by the induction and (
2.7
), (
2.8
) we get

\sum_{i=1}^{t}\overline{\pi^{(r+s+1)}_{i}}\leq\sum_{i=1}^{t}\overline{\sigma^{(r+s+1)}_{i}}.

If
j_{s}=l_{s}
we are done.

Let
j_{s}<j_{s}+1\leq t\leq l_{s}
.
By the choice of
j_{s},l_{s}
and the fact that

j_{s}<l_{s}
, we have
\overline{\pi_{i}^{(r+s)}}<\overline{\sigma_{i}^{(r+s)}}=r
for all
i=j_{s}+1,\ldots,l_{s}
. Moreover by the induction hypothesis we have
\sum_{i=1}^{j}\overline{\pi^{(r+s)}_{i}}\leq\sum_{i=1}^{j}\overline{\sigma^{(r+s)}_{i}},

for all
j
.
Therefore we get

\sum_{i=1}^{t}\overline{\pi^{(r+s)}_{i}}<\sum_{i=1}^{t}\overline{\sigma^{(r+s)}_{i}}.

Applying the formulae (
2.7
), (
2.8
) we deduce that
\sum_{i=1}^{t}\overline{\pi^{(r+s+1)}_{i}}\leq\sum_{i=1}^{t}\overline{\sigma^{(r+s+1)}_{i}}
for all
t
. Since the converse implication is obvious
we are done.

The statement 2 is obvious.

The statement 3 is a consequence of 1, 2, because by
[
16
, Theorem 1.1]
we have:

f(\Pi)\leq_{\rm dom}f(\Sigma)
if and only if

f(\Pi)\leq_{\rm box}f(\Sigma)
.

\square

3
SYTs and LR-tableaux

In order to apply orders
\leq_{\rm dom}
and
\leq_{\rm box}
to investigate properties of some objects of the category
\mathcal{S}
, in this section we adopt the results for SYTs presented in section
2
to the set of LR-tableaux that are rook strips.

Definition:

Let
(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)
be a partition triple. An
LR-tableau
of shape
\beta\setminus\gamma
and content
\alpha
is a Young diagram of shape
\beta

(the
outer shape
of the tableau) in which
the region
\beta\setminus\gamma
is filled with
\overline{\alpha}_{1}
entries
1,
\overline{\alpha}_{2}
entries 2, etc., such that the three conditions are satisfied:

1.

in each row the entries are weakly increasing;

2.

in each column the entries
are strictly increasing;

3.

the lattice permutation property holds, that is,
on the right hand side of each column there occur at least as many entries

e
as there are entries
e+1
(
e=1,2,\ldots
).

Remark:

An LR–tableau
\Gamma
of shape
\beta\setminus\gamma
and content
\alpha
can be identified with the
sequence

\Gamma=[\gamma^{(0)},\ldots,\gamma^{(|\alpha|)}]
of partitions such that

\gamma^{(0)}\subseteq\gamma^{(1)}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\gamma^{(|\alpha|)}
and
the skew diagram

\gamma^{(e)}\setminus\gamma^{(e-1)}
carries all entries
for all
e=1,\ldots,|\alpha|
and we set
\gamma^{(0)}=\gamma
,
\gamma^{|\alpha|}=\beta
.

Let
\alpha,\beta,\gamma
be partitions and
r\in\mathbb{N}
.
Throughout we assume that
\beta\setminus\gamma
is a rook strip. Let
\mathcal{T}_{\alpha,\gamma}^{\beta}
(resp.
\mathcal{T}_{r,\gamma}^{\beta}
) be the set of all LR-tableaux of type
(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)

(resp. the set of all LR-tableaux of type
(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)
such that
|\alpha|=r
). Note that

\mathcal{T}_{r,\gamma}^{\beta}=\bigcup^{\bullet}_{\alpha;|\alpha|=r}\mathcal{T}_{\alpha,\gamma}^{\beta}.

Box order and dominance order were defined on LR-tableaux in
[
14
]
.
We review these definitions.

Definition:

Let
\beta,\gamma
be partitions,
r=|\beta|-|\gamma|
and let
\Delta,\Gamma\in\mathcal{T}_{r,\gamma}^{\beta}
.

1.

The tableau
\Delta
is obtained by a
decreasing
box move
from the tableau

\Gamma
(we write
\Delta\prec_{\rm box}\Gamma
) if
\Delta
is obtained from
\Gamma
by swapping two entries, such that the
smaller entry winds up in the lower position (this move does not change the partition
\alpha
); or by increasing an entry (this move changes the partition
\alpha
). The box-order
\leq_{\rm box}
is the partial order
generated by the relation
\prec_{\rm box}
.

2.

Let
\Delta=[\delta^{(0)},\delta^{(1)},\ldots,\delta^{(r)}]\in\mathcal{T}_{\alpha^{\prime},\gamma}^{\beta},\;\Gamma=[\gamma^{(0)},\gamma^{(1)},\ldots,\gamma^{(r)}]\in\mathcal{T}_{\alpha,\gamma}^{\beta}
. We say that
\Delta\leq_{\rm dom}\Gamma
in the dominance order if

\delta^{(i)}\leq_{\rm nat}\gamma^{(i)}
for all
i=0,1,\ldots,r
.

Remark:

Note that for arbitrary
\Gamma=[\gamma^{(0)},\ldots,\gamma^{(r)}]\in\mathcal{T}_{\alpha,\gamma}^{\beta}
we have

\sum\limits_{i=0}^{c}\overline{\alpha}_{i}=|\gamma^{(c)}|-|\gamma|.

Therefore, if
\Delta\leq_{\rm dom}\Gamma
, then
\alpha\leq_{\rm nat}\alpha^{\prime}
.

Example:

Let

\Delta=\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\vtop{\halign{&\opttoksa@YT={\font@YT}\getcolor@YT{\save@YT{\opttoksb@YT}}\nil@YT\getcolor@YT{\startbox@@YT\the\opttoksa@YT\the\opttoksb@YT}#\endbox@YT\cr\lower height=8.4pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-8.4pt\hrule width=8.79993pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 8.00006pt\hrule width=8.79993pt,height=0.39993pt}&\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern be LR-tableaux of shape
\beta\setminus\gamma
and content
\alpha
and
\alpha^{\prime}
respectively for
\gamma=(4,3,2,1),\;\beta=(5,4,3,2,1),\;\alpha=(3,2)
and
\alpha^{\prime}=(2,2,1).

The LR-tableaux
\Delta,\Gamma
are identified with the
sequences

\Delta=[\delta^{(0)},\ldots,\delta^{(5)}],\Gamma=[\gamma^{(0)},\ldots,\gamma^{(5)}],

where
\delta^{(0)}=\gamma,\;\delta^{(1)}=(4,3,3,1,1),\;\delta^{(2)}=(5,3,3,2,1),\;\delta^{(3)}=\delta^{(4)}=\delta^{(5)}=\beta
and

\gamma^{(0)}=\gamma,\;\gamma^{(1)}=(4,3,3,2,1),\;\gamma^{(2)}=\gamma^{(3)}=\gamma^{(4)}=\gamma^{(5)}=\beta.

One can check that
\delta^{(i)}\leq_{\rm nat}\gamma^{(i)}
for all
i
, so
\Delta\leq_{\rm dom}\Gamma
. Moreover we have following sequence of decreasing box moves transforming tableau
\Gamma
into tableau

\Delta

so
\Delta\leq_{\rm box}\Gamma
.

In
[
16
, Section 3]
a bijection

\Phi^{\beta}_{\gamma}:\mathcal{T}_{\alpha,\gamma}^{\beta}\to\mathcal{T}_{\alpha}

is defined as follows.
For an LR-tableau
\Gamma
we denote by
\tau(\Gamma)=(\tau_{1},\ldots,\tau_{|\alpha|})
the
list of entries when
reading columns from the top down, starting with the rightmost
column and moving left. Fix
i\leq s
,
where
s
is
the number of rows of
\alpha
. Let
j_{1}<j_{2}<\ldots<j_{n_{i}}
be all elements
j
such that

\tau_{j}=i
. We write the
elements
j_{1},j_{2},\ldots,j_{n_{i}}

in the
i
-th row of the corresponding SYT of shape
\alpha
. This bijection has the property that given
\Delta,\Gamma\in\mathcal{T}_{\alpha,\gamma}^{\beta}
the relation
\Delta\leq_{\rm dom}\Gamma
(resp.
\Delta\leq_{\rm box}\Gamma
) holds if and only if

\Phi^{\beta}_{\gamma}(\Delta)\leq_{\rm dom}\Phi^{\beta}_{\gamma}(\Gamma)
(resp.
\Phi^{\beta}_{\gamma}(\Delta)\leq_{\rm box}\Phi^{\beta}_{\gamma}(\Gamma)
), see
[
16
, Lemma 3.1,Lemma 3.2]
.

The following example is presented in
[
16
]
. Consider LR-tableaux, which are related by the box move swapping the entries in rows 3 and 6.

height=8.4pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-8.4pt\hrule width=8.79993pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 8.00006pt\hrule width=8.79993pt,height=0.39993pt}&\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern height=8.4pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-8.4pt\hrule width=8.79993pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 8.00006pt\hrule width=8.79993pt,height=0.39993pt}\cr}}\kern 690.4pt}\kern 690.4pt}}$}

Starting from

\Phi^{\beta}_{\gamma}(\Gamma)
we can get
\Phi^{\beta}_{\gamma}(\Delta)
by
first swapping 3 and 4, then 4 and 6.

In the natural way we extend
\Phi_{\gamma}^{\beta}
to the bijection

\Phi^{\beta}_{\gamma}:\mathcal{T}_{r,\gamma}^{\beta}\to\mathcal{T}_{r}

(3.1)

Lemma 3.2
.

Given
\Delta,\Gamma\in\mathcal{T}_{r,\gamma}^{\beta}
:

1.

\Delta\leq_{\rm dom}\Gamma
if and only if
\Phi^{\beta}_{\gamma}(\Delta)\leq_{\rm dom}\Phi^{\beta}_{\gamma}(\Gamma)
,

2.

\Delta\leq_{\rm box}\Gamma
if and only if
\Phi^{\beta}_{\gamma}(\Delta)\leq_{\rm box}\Phi^{\beta}_{\gamma}(\Gamma)
.

Proof.

1.

Note that the sum of all entries not greater than
l
in the first
c
rows of
\Phi^{\beta}_{\gamma}(\Gamma)
is equal the sum of all non-empty boxes with entries not greater than
c
in the first
l^{\prime}
rows of
\Gamma
, where
l^{\prime}
is the number of row containing
l
-th non-empty box.
More precisely, for
\Gamma=[\lambda^{(0)},\ldots,\lambda^{(s)}]
and
\Phi^{\beta}_{\gamma}(\Gamma)=[\pi^{(0)},\ldots,\pi^{(s)}]
we have:

\sum\limits_{i=0}^{c}\overline{\pi}_{i}^{(l)}=\sum\limits_{i=0}^{l^{\prime}}\overline{\lambda}_{i}^{(c)}-\sum\limits_{i=0}^{l^{\prime}}\overline{\gamma}_{i}^{(c)},

Similarly for
\Delta
. The partition
\gamma
is the same for
\Gamma
and
\Delta
, so 1 follows.

2.

Let
\Delta,\Gamma\in\mathcal{T}_{r,\gamma}^{\beta}
. Suppose
\Delta
is obtained from
\Gamma
by a single decreasing box move. If
\Delta
is obtained from
\Gamma
by a swapping the positions of two entries, then
\Phi^{\beta}_{\gamma}(\Delta)\leq_{\rm box}\Phi^{\beta}_{\gamma}(\Gamma)
, by
[
16
, Lemma 3.1]
. Otherwise
\Delta
is obtained from
\Gamma
by increasing an entry. Suppose that the entry
\tau_{s}=e
in
\Gamma
is replied by the entry
\tau_{s}=f
in
\Delta
for some
e<f
.

By the definition of
\Phi^{\beta}_{\gamma}
we obtain
\Phi^{\beta}_{\gamma}(\Delta)
from
\Phi^{\beta}_{\gamma}(\Gamma)
by winding up the
entry
from the row
e
to the row
f

and resorting the entries in rows
f
and
e
if necessary. This resorting step is not allowed in our definition of decreasing box moves for SYTs.

We show that there exists a sequence of admissible decreasing box moves which transform
\Phi^{\beta}_{\gamma}(\Gamma)
into
\Phi^{\beta}_{\gamma}(\Delta)
.

If the row
f
in
\Phi^{\beta}_{\gamma}(\Gamma)
is empty, we can wind up the last entry of the row
e
to the row
f
and go to third step in the procedure below. Otherwise, let the entry
be the largest entry in row
f
of
\Phi^{\beta}_{\gamma}(\Gamma)
. If
s>j
, then we can wind up the entry
after the last entry of the row
f
and get
\Phi^{\beta}_{\gamma}(\Delta)
. Otherwise
s<j
and we divide the procedure into three steps.

First step.
In the LR-tableau
\Gamma
we swap the entry
\tau_{j}=f
with
\tau_{s}=e
. Since
\Delta
and
\Gamma
are LR-tableaux, we get an LR-tableau
\Gamma^{\prime}
. Note that
\Pi=\Phi^{\beta}_{\gamma}(\Gamma^{\prime})
is obtained from
\Phi^{\beta}_{\gamma}(\Gamma)
by swapping the entry
with the entry
in and resorting the entries in rows
f
and
e
if necessary. We reduced the problem to
[
16
, Lemma 3.1]
. Therefore this transformation can be realized by execute the sequence of swapping two entries in
\Phi^{\beta}_{\gamma}(\Gamma)
so that the smaller entry winds up in the higher–numbered row (for the procedure see the proof of
[
16
, Lemma 3.1]
).

Second step.
Note that in the tableau
\Pi
obtained from
\Phi^{\beta}_{\gamma}(\Gamma)
in the first step the last entry of row
e
(that is bigger or equal than
j
) is greater than the last entry of row
f
(because
j
was the largest entry in row
f
before swapping). So we can execute decreasing box move to
\Pi
which winding up the last box of the
e
-th row to the last position of the
f
-th row in this tableau.

Third step.
If the entry winded up to the
f
-th row is equal to
,
then tableau
\Pi^{\prime}
obtained in this step is
\Phi^{\beta}_{\gamma}(\Delta)
and we are done. Otherwise we obtain
\Phi^{\beta}_{\gamma}(\Delta)
by swapping this entry and the box
in the same way as in the first step.

The fact that all the tableaux we pass through are indeed standard, follows from the described procedure and the fact that the first and last tableaux are standard.

We showed that if
\Delta
is obtained from
\Gamma
by a single decreasing box move, then
\Phi^{\beta}_{\gamma}(\Delta)\leq_{\rm box}\Phi^{\beta}_{\gamma}(\Gamma).
By the definition of box orders
\Delta\leq_{\rm box}\Gamma
implies
\Phi^{\beta}_{\gamma}(\Delta)\leq_{\rm box}\Phi^{\beta}_{\gamma}(\Gamma).

It is easy to see that the converse implication is also true: if there is a decreasing box move from
\Phi^{\beta}_{\gamma}(\Gamma)
to
\Phi^{\beta}_{\gamma}(\Delta)
, then the corresponding move from
\Gamma
to
\Delta
is a decreasing box move. Indeed, if
\Phi^{\beta}_{\gamma}(\Gamma)
and
\Phi^{\beta}_{\gamma}(\Delta)
have the same shape, it follows from
[
16
]
; otherwise the decreasing box move from
\Phi^{\beta}_{\gamma}(\Gamma)
to
\Phi^{\beta}_{\gamma}(\Delta)
implies that
\Delta
is obtained from
\Gamma
by increasing an entry.

\square

To illustrate procedure described in the proof
consider the following LR-tableaux, which are related by the decreasing box move which increases entry
in row 3.

The winding up the entry
of
\Phi^{\beta}_{\gamma}(\Gamma)
from the first row to the third row is not a legal decreasing box move. But
\Phi^{\beta}_{\gamma}(\Delta)
can be obtain from
\Phi^{\beta}_{\gamma}(\Gamma)
by the sequence of the following decreasing box moves:

1.

swap
6
and
4
,

2.

swap
4
and
3
,

3.

wind up box
to the third row,

4.

swap
9
and
8
,

5.

swap
8
and
7

6.

swap
7
and
6
.

The points 1. and 2. correspond to the first step described in the proof, the point 3. corresponds to the second step and the points 4., 5., 6. correspond to the last step.

4
Invariant subspaces of nilpotent linear operators

Let
k
be a field and let
\Lambda=k[p]
be the algebra of polynomials with one variable
p
.
By
\mbox{{\rm mod}}_{0}\Lambda
we denote the category of all nilpotent linear operators, i.e.
\Lambda
-modules is isomorphic to

N_{\alpha}=N_{\alpha}(\Lambda)=\Lambda/(p^{\alpha_{1}})\oplus\Lambda/(p^{\alpha_{2}})\oplus\ldots\oplus\Lambda/(p^{\alpha_{n}}),

where
\alpha_{1}\geq\alpha_{2}\geq\ldots\geq\alpha_{n}
.
The function

N_{\alpha}\mapsto\alpha=(\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{n})
defines a bijection
between the set of all isomorphism classes of nilpotent finite dimensional
\Lambda
-modules
and the set
\mathcal{P}
of all partitions.

4.1
Invariant subspaces and LR-tableaux

Let
\mathcal{S}(\Lambda)
be the category of all short exact sequences of modules
in
\mbox{{\rm mod}}_{0}\Lambda
with morphisms given by commutative diagrams.
With the componentwise exact structure,

\mathcal{S}(\Lambda)
is an exact Krull-Remak-Schmidt category, see
[
23
]
.
For brevity we sometimes use notation:
A=N_{\alpha}
,
B=N_{\beta}
,
C=N_{\gamma}
and objects in
\mathcal{S}(\Lambda)
, that is short exact sequences

0\to A\to B\to C\to 0
of
\Lambda
-modules, we denote as embeddings

(A\subset B)
.

An embedding
(A\subset B)
with corresponding short exact
sequence
0\to A\to B\to C\to 0
yields three partitions

\alpha,\beta,\gamma
which describe the isomorphism types of

A,B,C
, respectively.
We call this partition triple
(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)

type
of the embedding
(A\subset B)
(or equivalently of the corresponding short exact sequence).
The Green-Klein Theorem
[
20
,
10
]
states that a partition triple

(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)
is the type of a short exact
sequence in
\mathcal{S}(\Lambda)
if and only if there exists an LR-tableau
of shape
\beta\setminus\gamma
and content
\alpha
.
The tableau of an embedding can be obtained in the following way.

Definition:

Let
X=(A\subset B)
be an object of
\mathcal{S}(\Lambda)
. Suppose that the submodule
A
of
B
has Loewy
length
r
. Then the sequence of epimorphisms

B=B/p^{r}A\twoheadrightarrow B/p^{r-1}A\twoheadrightarrow\cdots\twoheadrightarrow B/pA\twoheadrightarrow B/A

induces a sequence of inclusions of partitions

\beta=\gamma^{(r)}\supset\gamma^{(r-1)}\supset\cdots\supset\gamma^{(1)}\supset\gamma^{(0)}=\gamma,

where
N_{\gamma^{(e)}}\simeq B/p^{e}A
. By
[
20
, II(3.4)]
the tableau
[\gamma^{(0)},\ldots,\gamma^{(r)}]
is an LR-tableau. The partitions define the corresponding LR-tableau

LR(X)=\Gamma=[\gamma^{(0)},\gamma^{(1)},\ldots,\gamma^{(r)}]

of
X.

The
union
of two tableaux is taken row-wise, so if

{\rm E}=[\varepsilon^{(i)}]_{0\leq i\leq s}
and
{\rm Z}=[\zeta^{(i)}]_{0\leq i\leq t}

are tableaux then
the partitions
\gamma^{(i)}
for
\Gamma={\rm E}\cup{\rm Z}
are given by taking
the union (of ordered multi-sets):

\gamma^{(i)}=\varepsilon^{(i)}\cup\zeta^{(i)}

where
\varepsilon^{(i)}=\varepsilon^{(s)}

for
i\geq s
and
\zeta^{(i)}=\zeta^{(t)}
for
i\geq t
.

Remark:

It is easy to observe that if embeddings
(A\subset B)
,
(C\subset D)

have tableaux
\Gamma_{1}
,
\Gamma_{2}
, respectively, then the direct sum

(A\subset B)\oplus(C\subset D)
has tableau

\Gamma=\Gamma_{1}\cup\Gamma_{2}
.

4.2
Two types of invariant subspaces

First we review Kaplansky’s classification of cyclic embeddings, given in
[
9
]
.

We call an embedding
(A\subset B)

cyclic

if the submodule
A
is a cyclic
\Lambda
-module,
that is, if
A
is either indecomposable (i.e. of the form
\Lambda/(p^{n})
for some
n\geq 1
) or zero.
A cyclic embedding
(A\subset B)

is called a
pole
if
A
is an indecomposable
\Lambda
-module
and if
(A\subset B)
is an indecomposable object in
\mathcal{S}(\Lambda)
.
An embedding of the form
(0\subset B)
is called
empty.

By
E_{\beta}
we denote the empty embedding

(0\subset N_{\beta})
.

Following
[
9
]
(see also
[
13
]
) we use height sequences to give classification of poles.

Definition:

1.

A
height sequence
is a sequence in
\mathbb{N}_{0}\cup\{\infty\}

which is strictly increasing and has finitely many elements from
\mathbb{N}_{0}
.
A height sequence is
non-empty
, if it has at least
one element from
\mathbb{N}_{0}
.

2.

An element
a\in B\in\mbox{{\rm mod}}\Lambda
has
height

m

if
a\in p^{m}B\setminus p^{m+1}B
.
In this case we write
h(a)=m
and set
h(0)=\infty
.

3.

The
height sequence for
a
in
B
is

H_{B}(a)=(h(p^{i}a))_{i\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}
.
Usually, we do not write the entries
\infty
.

4.

A sequence

(m_{i})
has a
gap
after
m_{\ell}
if
m_{\ell+1}>m_{\ell}+1
.

The following result is proved in
[
9
, Theorem 25]
.

Proposition 4.1
.

There is a bijection

\{\text{poles}\}/_{\cong}\quad\stackrel{{\scriptstyle 1-1}}{{\longleftrightarrow}}\quad\{\text{finite non-empty strictly increasing sequences in $\mathbb{N}_{0}$}\}.

Example:

Let
m=(m_{i})_{0\leq i\leq n}
be a strictly increasing
sequence in
\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Following
[
9
]
,
we describe a cyclic embedding
(A\subset B)
corresponding to
m
under the bijection in Proposition
4.1
.
Let
i_{1}>i_{2}>\cdots>i_{s}
be such that

(m_{i})
has gaps exactly after the entries

m_{i_{1}}>m_{i_{2}}>\cdots>m_{i_{s}}
.
For
1\leq j\leq s
put
\beta_{j}=m_{i_{j}}+1

and
\ell_{j}=m_{i_{j}}-i_{j}
, then
\beta
and
\ell
are strictly decreasing
sequences of positive and nonnegative integers, respectively.
Let

B=N_{\beta}=\bigoplus_{i=1}^{s}\Lambda/(p^{\beta_{i}})

be generated by elements
b_{\beta_{j}}
of order
p^{\beta_{j}}
.
Let
a=\sum_{j=1}^{s}p^{\ell_{j}}\cdot b_{\beta_{j}}
and put
A=(a)
.
This gives a cyclic embedding
P((m_{i}))=(A\subset B)
.

Example:

The following example is taken from
[
13
]
.
The height sequence
(1,3,4)
has gaps after
1
and
4
, and
hence gives rise to the embedding

P((1,3,4))=((p^{2}b_{5}+pb_{2})\subset N_{(5,2)})
.

Lemma 4.2
.

Suppose the height sequence
(m_{i})
of some element
a
in
B
has a gap after
m_{\ell}
.
Then
N_{(m_{\ell}+1)}
occurs as a direct summand of
B
.

Proof of the lemma.

See
[
9
, Lemma 22 and page 29]
.

\square

The following facts were proved in
[
15
, Proposition 2.19]
.

Proposition 4.3
.

Suppose
(A\subset B)
is a cyclic embedding with
A

of Loewy length
r
. Let
\Gamma
be an LR-tableau of this embedding.

1.

In the tableau, each entry
1,\ldots,r

occurs exactly once.

2.

The height sequence
(m_{i})

of the submodule generator
a

determines the rows in
\Gamma
in which
the entries occur, and conversely.
More precisely, the entry
e
occurs in row
m_{e-1}+1
.

We define the second type of embeddings. Let
r>q
be natural numbers and
m=(m_{0},\ldots,m_{r})
,
n=(n_{0},\ldots,n_{q})
be two strictly increasing sequences with gaps exactly after
m_{i_{1}}>\ldots>m_{i_{s}}
and
n_{l_{1}}>\ldots>n_{l_{t}}
respectively. Assume that
m_{r}>n_{q}+1
. Let
D(m,n)
be the embedding defined as follows.
For
1\leq j\leq s
(respectively
1\leq j^{\prime}\leq t
) put
\beta_{j}=m_{i_{j}}+1
(resp.
\lambda_{j^{\prime}}=n_{l_{j^{\prime}}}+1
) and
\ell_{j}=m_{i_{j}}-i_{j}
(resp.
\ell^{\prime}_{j^{\prime}}=n_{l_{j^{\prime}}}-l_{j^{\prime}}
), then
\beta
(resp.
\lambda
) and
\ell
(resp.
\ell^{\prime}
) are strictly decreasing
sequences of positive and nonnegative integers, respectively.

Let

N_{\beta}=\bigoplus_{i=1}^{s}\Lambda/(p^{\beta_{i}})\;\;({\rm resp.}\;\;N_{\lambda}=\bigoplus_{i=1}^{t}\Lambda/(p^{\lambda_{i}})

be generated by elements
b_{\beta_{j}}
of order
p^{\beta_{j}}
and
b^{\prime}_{\lambda_{j}}
of order
p^{\lambda_{j}}
, respectively.
Let
a_{1}=\sum_{j=1}^{s}p^{\ell_{j}}\cdot b_{\beta_{j}}
and
a_{2}=\sum_{j=2}^{s}p^{\ell_{j}+r-q-1}\cdot b_{\beta_{j}}+\sum_{j=1}^{t}p^{\ell^{\prime}_{j}}\cdot b^{\prime}_{\lambda_{j}}
then
D(m,n)=((a_{1},a_{2})\subset N_{\beta}\oplus N_{\lambda})
.

Lemma 4.4
.

Let
r>q
be natural numbers and let
m=(m_{0},\ldots,m_{r})
and
n=(n_{0},\ldots,n_{q})
be two strictly increasing sequences with gaps exactly after
m_{i_{1}}>\ldots>m_{i_{s}}
and
n_{l_{1}}>\ldots>n_{l_{t}}
respectively. If
m_{r}\geq n_{q}+1
, then the embeddings
D(m,n)
and
P(m)\oplus P(n)
have the same LR-tableau.

Proof.

To prove that
D(m,n)
and
P(m)\oplus P(n)
have the same LR-tableau we apply the following observation, see
[
14
, (2.4)]
.
For any embedding
(A\subset B)
with LR-tableau
[\gamma^{(i)}]
, the number of boxes in the first
w
rows of
\gamma^{(e)}
is the length of the
\Lambda
-module
B/(p^{e}A+p^{w}B)
.

Let
r>q
be natural numbers and let
m=(m_{0},\ldots,m_{r})
,
n=(n_{0},\ldots,n_{q})
be two strictly increasing sequences such that
m_{r}>n_{q}+1
with gaps exactly after
m_{i_{1}}>\ldots>m_{i_{s}}
and
n_{l_{1}}>\ldots>n_{l_{t}}
respectively. Put
\beta_{j}=m_{i_{j}}+1
(resp.
\lambda_{j^{\prime}}=n_{l_{j^{\prime}}}+1
) and
\ell_{j}=m_{i_{j}}-i_{j}
(resp.
\ell^{\prime}_{j^{\prime}}=n_{l_{j^{\prime}}}-l_{j^{\prime}}
).
Let
a_{1}=\sum_{j=1}^{s}p^{\ell_{j}}\cdot b_{\beta_{j}}
,
a_{2}=\sum_{j=1}^{t}p^{\ell^{\prime}_{j}}\cdot b^{\prime}_{\lambda_{j}}
,
a_{3}=\sum_{j=2}^{s}p^{\ell_{j}+r-q-1}\cdot b_{\beta_{j}}
,
P(m)\oplus P(n)=(A=(a_{1},a_{2})\subset N_{\beta}\oplus N_{\lambda})
and
D(m,n)=(A_{1}=(a_{1},a_{3}+a_{2})\subset N_{\beta}\oplus N_{\lambda})
. Denote
B=N_{\beta}\oplus N_{\lambda}
.

We prove that the dimension of
p^{e}A
is equal to the dimension of
p^{e}A_{1}
and the dimension of
p^{e}A\cap p^{w}B
is equal to the dimension of
p^{e}A_{1}\cap p^{w}B
, for all
e,w
.

Since
i_{j}>i_{j+1}
,
l_{j}>l_{j+1}
,for all
j
, we have

(P1)

m_{i_{1}}-\ell_{i_{1}}>m_{i_{j}}-\ell_{i_{j}}
, for all
j
;

(P2)

n_{l_{1}}-\ell^{\prime}_{l_{1}}>n_{l_{j}}-\ell^{\prime}_{l_{j}}
, for all
j
.

Moreover we have:

(P3)

\lambda_{1}-\ell_{1}^{\prime}\geq\beta_{2}-(\ell_{2}+r-q-1)
.

Indeed, it is easy to check that
\lambda_{1}-\ell^{\prime}_{1}=l_{1}+1
,
\beta_{2}-\ell_{2}-r=1+i_{2}-i_{1}\leq 0
and
\beta_{2}-(\ell_{2}+r-q-1)\leq\ell_{1}+1
. Combining these we get (P3).

By (P1) and (P2) the elements
p^{e}a_{1},\ldots,p^{m_{i_{1}}-\ell_{1}+1}a_{1}
,
p^{e}a_{2},\ldots,p^{n_{l_{1}}-\ell_{1}^{\prime}+1}a_{2}
form a basis of
p^{e}A
. Moreover, by (P1), (P2) and (P3), the elements
p^{e}a_{1},\ldots,\allowbreak p^{m_{i_{1}}-\ell_{1}+1}a_{1}
,
p^{e}(a_{3}+a_{2}),\ldots,p^{n_{l_{1}}-\ell_{1}^{\prime}+1}(a_{3}+a_{2})
form a basis of
p^{e}A_{1}
. It follows that dimensions of
p^{e}A
and
p^{e}A_{1}
are equal.

Note that, for suitable chosen
c,\overline{c}
, the elements
p^{c}a_{1},\ldots,p^{m_{i_{1}}-\ell_{1}+1}a_{1}
,
p^{\overline{c}}a_{2},\ldots,\allowbreak p^{n_{l_{1}}-\ell_{1}^{\prime}+1}a_{2}
form a basis of
p^{e}A\cap p^{w}B
. It follows that if
p^{\overline{c}}a_{2}\neq 0
, then
\overline{c}+\ell_{1}^{\prime}\geq w
.

To construct a basis of
p^{e}A_{1}\cap p^{w}B
, note that

(P4)

x+\ell_{1}+r-q-1\geq w
, for
x\geq\overline{c}
.

Indeed, since

\ell_{1}+r-q-1=m_{r}-r+r-q-1=m_{r}-q-1\geq n_{q}-q=\ell_{1}^{\prime}

we have
x+\ell_{1}+r-q-1\geq x+\ell_{1}^{\prime}\geq w
.

Let
x\geq\overline{c}
. Assume that
0\neq p^{x}(a_{2}+a_{3})\not\in p^{w}B
. By (P3) and (P4), we have
p^{x}(a_{3}+a_{2}-p^{r-q-1}a_{1})\in p^{w}B
.

Finally
p^{c}a_{1},\ldots,p^{m_{i_{1}}-\ell_{1}+1}a_{1}
,
p^{\overline{c}}a^{\overline{c}}_{4},\ldots,\allowbreak p^{n_{l_{1}}-\ell_{1}^{\prime}+1}a^{n_{l_{1}}-\ell_{1}^{\prime}+1}_{4}
is a basis of
p^{e}A_{1}\cap p^{w}B
, where
a^{y}_{4}=a_{3}+a_{2}
, if
p^{y}(a_{3}+a_{2})\in p^{w}B
; and
a^{y}_{4}=a_{3}+a_{2}-p^{r-q-1}a_{1}
otherwise.

\square

5
Algebraic partial orders

We show how partial orders
\leq_{\rm box}
and
\leq_{\rm dom}
control some algebraic properties of the category
\mathcal{S}(\Lambda)
. For this we compare these orders with the two classical partial orders

\leq_{\sf ext},\quad\leq_{\sf hom}

that have been studied extensively for modules over finite dimensional algebras, see for example
[
5
,
4
,
22
,
11
,
29
]
.
These partial orders are also investigated for the category
\mathcal{S}(\Lambda)
, see
[
12
,
14
]
.

Let
\alpha,\beta,\gamma
be a triple of partitions,
r\in\mathbb{N}
and let
\mathcal{S}_{\alpha,\gamma}^{\beta}
(resp.
\mathcal{S}_{r,\gamma}^{\beta}
) be the full subcategory of
\mathcal{S}(\Lambda)
consisting
of objects
(N_{\alpha}\subseteq N_{\beta})
such that

N_{\beta}/N_{\alpha}\simeq N_{\gamma}
(resp.
N_{\beta}/N_{\alpha}\simeq N_{\gamma}
and
|\alpha|=r
). Note that
X
is in
\mathcal{S}_{\alpha,\gamma}^{\beta}
(resp. in
\mathcal{S}_{r,\gamma}^{\beta}
)
if and only if
LR(X)
is in
\mathcal{T}_{\alpha,\gamma}^{\beta}
(resp. in
\mathcal{T}_{r,\gamma}^{\beta}
).

The following definitions for
\mathcal{T}_{\alpha,\gamma}^{\beta}

are introduced in
[
14
]
. We generalize them to
\mathcal{T}_{r,\gamma}^{\beta}
.

Definition:

Let
X,Y\in\mathcal{S}_{r,\gamma}^{\beta}
.

1.

The relation
X\leq_{\sf ext}Y
holds, if there exist
embeddings
H_{i}
,
U_{i}
,
V_{i}
in
\mathcal{S}(\Lambda)
and short exact
sequences
0\to U_{i}\to H_{i}\to V_{i}\to 0
in
\mathcal{S}(\Lambda)

such that
X\cong H_{1}
,
U_{i}\oplus V_{i}\cong H_{i+1}
for
1\leq i\leq s
,
and
Y\cong H_{s+1}
, for some natural
number
s
.

2.

The relation
X\leq_{\sf hom}Y
holds
if

[X,Z]\leq[YZ]

for any embedding
Z
in
\mathcal{S}=\mathcal{S}(\Lambda)
,
where
[X,Z]
denotes the dimension of the linear space
\mbox{\rm Hom}_{\mathcal{S}}(X,Z)

of all homomorphisms of embeddings.

These orders induce two reflexive and anti-symmetric relations on the set
\mathcal{T}_{r,\gamma}^{\beta}
.

Definition:

Suppose
\Gamma
,
\widetilde{\Gamma}
are two LR-tableaux in
\mathcal{T}_{r,\gamma}^{\beta}
.
We write
\Gamma\leq_{\sf ext}\widetilde{\Gamma}

(resp.
\Gamma\leq_{\sf hom}\widetilde{\Gamma}
)
if there is a sequence

\Gamma=\Gamma^{(0)},\Gamma^{(1)},\ldots,\Gamma^{(s)}=\widetilde{\Gamma}

such that for each
1\leq i\leq s
there are
X,Y\in\mathcal{S}_{r,\gamma}^{\beta}
with
LR(X)=\Gamma^{(i-1)}
,

LR(Y)=\Gamma^{(i)}
and
X\leq_{\sf ext}Y

(resp.
X\leq_{\sf hom}Y
).

The following lemma follows from the corresponding properties for modules. One can it also easily prove applying the functor
Hom_{\mathcal{S}}(-,Z)
to the short exact sequences given in the definition of
\leq_{\rm ext}
.

Lemma 5.1
.

Suppose
\Gamma
,
\widetilde{\Gamma}
are two LR-tableaux in
\mathcal{T}_{r,\gamma}^{\beta}
. If

\Gamma\leq_{\sf ext}\widetilde{\Gamma}
then
\Gamma\leq_{\sf hom}\widetilde{\Gamma}
.

In the following proposition we show that the hom-relation implies the dominance order.
As a consequence, the relations

\leq_{\sf ext},\;\leq_{\sf hom}
are anti-symmetric, hence partial orders.

Proposition 5.2
.

Let
\Delta
and
\Gamma
be LR-tableaux in
\mathcal{T}_{r,\gamma}^{\beta}
. If

\Delta\leq_{\rm hom}\Gamma
, then
\Delta\leq_{\rm dom}\Gamma
.

Proof.

By the definition of hom–order there is a sequence

\Delta=\Delta^{(0)},\Delta^{(1)},\ldots,\Delta^{(s)}=\Gamma

such that for each
0\leq i\leq s-1
there are
X,Y\in\mathcal{S}_{r,\gamma}^{\beta}
with
LR(X)=\Delta^{(i)}
,

LR(Y)=\Delta^{(i+1)}
and
X\leq_{\rm hom}Y
. Suppose that
X=(A\subset B)
and
Y=(\widetilde{A}\subset\widetilde{B})
are such that
(A\subset B)\leq_{\rm hom}(\widetilde{A}\subset\widetilde{B})
and that they have LR-tableaux
\Delta^{(i)},\Delta^{(i+1)}
, respectively, for some
0\leq i\leq s-1
.

From the formula given in the proof of
[
14
, Lemma 4.2]
it follows:

\overline{\gamma^{(i)}_{1}}+\cdots+\overline{\gamma^{(i)}_{\ell}}=\dim\mbox{\rm Hom}_{\mathcal{N}}(B/T^{i}A,N_{(\ell)})=\dim\mbox{\rm Hom}_{\mathcal{S}}((A\subset B),P_{i}^{\ell}),

\overline{\delta^{(i)}_{1}}+\cdots+\overline{\delta^{(i)}_{\ell}}=\dim\mbox{\rm Hom}_{\mathcal{N}}(\widetilde{B}/T^{i}\widetilde{A},N_{(\ell)})=\dim\mbox{\rm Hom}_{\mathcal{S}}((\widetilde{A}\subset\widetilde{B}),P_{i}^{\ell}),

where
\Delta^{(i)}=[\gamma^{(1)},\ldots,\gamma^{(r)}]
and
\Delta^{(i+1)}=[\delta^{(1)},\ldots,\delta^{(r)}].
Since
\Delta^{(i)}\leq_{\rm hom}\Delta^{(i+1)}
, we get

\overline{\gamma^{(i)}_{1}}+\cdots+\overline{\gamma^{(i)}_{\ell}}=\dim\mbox{\rm Hom}_{\mathcal{S}}((A\subset B),P_{i}^{\ell})\\
\leq\dim\mbox{\rm Hom}_{\mathcal{S}}((\widetilde{A}\subset\widetilde{B}),P_{i}^{\ell})=\overline{\delta^{(i)}_{1}}+\cdots+\overline{\delta^{(i)}_{\ell}}.

It follows that
\Delta^{(i)}\leq_{\rm dom}\Delta^{(i+1)}
for
0\leq i\leq s
, and finally that
\Delta\leq_{\rm dom}\Gamma
.

\square

6
The box-relation implies the-ext relation

Lemma 6.1
.

Let
r>q
be natural numbers and let
m=(m_{0},\ldots,m_{r})
and
n=(n_{0},\ldots,n_{q})
be two strictly increasing sequences with gaps exactly after
m_{i_{1}}>\ldots>m_{i_{s}}
and
n_{l_{1}}>\ldots>n_{l_{t}}
respectively. If
i_{2}=r-1
,
m_{r}>n_{q}+1
, then there exists an exact sequence:

0\to P(n^{\prime})\to D(m,n)\to P(m^{\prime})\to 0,

where
n^{\prime}=(n_{0},n_{1},\ldots,n_{q},m_{r})
and
m^{\prime}=(m_{0},\ldots,m_{r-1})
.

Proof.

Set
\beta_{j}=m_{i_{j}}+1
and
\lambda_{j^{\prime}}=n_{l_{j^{\prime}}}+1
. Note that
i_{1}=r
and that the sequences
m^{\prime}
,
n^{\prime}
have gaps exactly after
m_{i_{2}}>\ldots>m_{i_{s}}
and
m_{r}>n_{l_{1}}>\ldots>n_{l_{t}}
respectively. Under the assumptions of lemma we have

P(n^{\prime})=((p^{m_{r}-(q+1)}\cdot b_{\beta_{1}}+\sum_{j=1}^{t}p^{\ell^{\prime}_{j}}\cdot b^{\prime}_{\lambda_{j}})\subset N_{(m_{r}+1,n_{l_{1}}+1,n_{l_{2}}+1,\ldots,n_{l_{t}}+1)}),

D(n,m)=((\sum_{j=1}^{s}p^{\ell_{j}}\cdot b_{\beta_{j}},\sum_{j=2}^{s}p^{\ell_{j}+r-q-1}\cdot b_{\beta_{j}}+\sum_{j=1}^{t}p^{\ell^{\prime}_{j}}\cdot b^{\prime}_{\lambda_{j}})\subset\\
\subset N_{(m_{i_{1}}+1,m_{i_{2}}+1,\ldots,m_{i_{s}}+1)}\oplus N_{(n_{l_{1}}+1,n_{l_{2}}+1,\ldots,n_{l_{t}}+1)})

and

P(m^{\prime})=((\sum_{j=2}^{s}p^{\ell_{j}}\cdot b_{\beta_{j}})\subset N_{(m_{i_{2}}+1,\ldots,m_{i_{s}}+1)}),

where
\ell_{j}=m_{i_{j}}-i_{j}
and
\ell^{\prime}_{j^{\prime}}=n_{l_{j^{\prime}}}-l_{j^{\prime}}
, for
1\leq j\leq s
and
1\leq j^{\prime}\leq t.

Since
i_{1}=r
,
i_{2}=r-1
and
m_{r}>n_{q}+1
, it is straightforward to check that the following sequence is exact.

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{N_{\lambda\cup(\beta_{1})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Psi}
\textstyle{N_{\beta}\oplus N_{\lambda}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Phi}
\textstyle{N_{\beta\setminus(\beta_{1})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{N_{(q+2)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\left[\begin{smallmatrix}-p^{r-q-1}\\
1\end{smallmatrix}\right]}
\textstyle{N_{(r+1,q+1)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\left[\begin{smallmatrix}1&p^{r-q-1}\end{smallmatrix}\right]}
\textstyle{N_{(r)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{3}}
\textstyle{0,}

where

f_{1}=\left[\begin{smallmatrix}p^{m_{r}-(q+1)}&p^{n_{l_{1}}-l_{1}}&\ldots&p^{n_{l_{t}}-l_{t}}\end{smallmatrix}\right]^{T},

f_{3}=\left[\begin{smallmatrix}p^{m_{i_{2}}-i_{2}}&\ldots&p^{m_{i_{s}}-i_{s}}\end{smallmatrix}\right]^{T},

f_{2}=\left[\begin{smallmatrix}p^{m_{i_{1}}-i_{1}}&p^{m_{i_{2}}-i_{2}}&\ldots&p^{m_{i_{s}}-i_{s}}&0&\ldots&0\\
0&p^{m_{i_{2}}-i_{2}+r-q-1}&\ldots&p^{m_{i_{s}}-i_{s}+r-q-1}&p^{n_{l_{1}}-l_{1}}&\ldots&p^{n_{l_{t}}-l_{t}}\end{smallmatrix}\right]^{T}

and

\Psi=\left[\begin{array}[]{c | c}-1&\begin{array}[]{c c c}0&\ldots&0\end{array}\\
\hline\cr 0&\begin{array}[]{c c c}0&\ldots&0\end{array}\\
\vdots&\begin{array}[]{c c c}\vdots&&\vdots\end{array}\\
0&\begin{array}[]{c c c}0&\ldots&0\end{array}\\
\hline\cr 0&Id_{N_{\lambda\cup(\beta_{1})}}\end{array}\right],\quad\quad\Phi=\left[\begin{array}[]{c | c | c |c}\begin{array}[]{c}0\\
\vdots\\
0\end{array}&\begin{array}[]{c}\\
Id_{N_{\lambda\setminus(\beta_{1})}}\\
\end{array}&\begin{array}[]{c c c}0&\ldots&0\\
\vdots&&\vdots\\
0&\ldots&0\end{array}\end{array}\right]

\square

Lemma 6.2
.

Let
r>q
be the natural numbers and let
m=(m_{0},\ldots,m_{r})
and
n=(n_{0},\ldots,n_{q})
be two strictly increasing sequences with gaps exactly after
m_{i_{1}}>\ldots>m_{i_{s}}
and
n_{l_{1}}>\ldots>n_{l_{t}}
respectively. If
i_{2}\neq r-1
,
m_{r}>n_{q}+1
, then there exists an exact sequence:

0\to P(n^{\prime})\to D(m,n)\oplus E_{m_{r}}\to P(m^{\prime})\to 0,

where
n^{\prime}=(n_{0},n_{1},\ldots,n_{q},m_{r})
and
m^{\prime}=(m_{0},\ldots,m_{r-1})
.

Proof.

Fix
\beta_{j}=m_{i_{j}}+1
and
\lambda_{j}=n_{l_{j}}+1
.
Note that
m_{r-1}=m_{r}-1
(so
m_{r-1}-(r-1)=m_{r}-r
) and that the sequences
m^{\prime}
,
n^{\prime}
have gaps exactly after
m_{r-1}>m_{i_{2}}>\ldots>m_{i_{s}}
and
m_{r}>n_{l_{1}}>\ldots>n_{l_{t}}
respectively. Under assumptions of lemma we have

P(n^{\prime})=((p^{m_{r}-(q+1)}\cdot b_{m_{r}+1}+\sum_{j=1}^{t}p^{\ell^{\prime}_{j}}\cdot b^{\prime}_{n_{l_{j}}+1})\subset N_{(m_{r}+1,n_{l_{1}}+1,n_{l_{2}}+1,\ldots,n_{l_{t}}+1)}),

D(n,m)\oplus E_{m_{r}}=((\sum_{j=1}^{s}p^{\ell_{j}}\cdot b_{\beta_{j}},\sum_{j=2}^{s}p^{\ell_{j}+r-q-1}\cdot b_{\beta_{j}}+\sum_{j=1}^{t}p^{\ell^{\prime}_{j}}\cdot b^{\prime}_{\lambda_{j}})\subset\\
\subset N_{(m_{i_{1}}+1,m_{i_{2}}+1,\ldots,m_{i_{s}}+1)}\oplus N_{(n_{l_{1}}+1,n_{l_{2}}+1,\ldots,n_{l_{t}}+1)})

and

P(m^{\prime})=((p^{m_{r}-r}\cdot b_{m_{r}}+\sum_{j=2}^{s}p^{\ell_{j}}\cdot b_{m_{i_{j}}+1})\subset N_{(m_{r},m_{i_{2}}+1,\ldots,m_{i_{s}}+1)}),

where
\ell_{j}=m_{i_{j}}-i_{j}
and
\ell^{\prime}_{j^{\prime}}=n_{l_{j^{\prime}}}-l_{j^{\prime}}
for
1\leq j\leq s
and
1\leq j^{\prime}\leq t.

Since
m_{r-1}-(r-1)=m_{r}-r
,
i_{1}=r
and
m_{r}>n_{q}+1
it is straightforward
to check that the following sequence is exact.

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{N_{(n^{\prime})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Psi}
\textstyle{N_{(m)}\oplus N_{(n)}\oplus N_{(m_{r})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Phi}
\textstyle{N_{(m^{\prime})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{N_{(q+2)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\left[\begin{smallmatrix}-p^{r-q-1}\\
1\end{smallmatrix}\right]}
\textstyle{N_{(r+1,q+1)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\left[\begin{smallmatrix}1&p^{r-q-1}\end{smallmatrix}\right]}
\textstyle{N_{(r)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{3}}
\textstyle{0,}

where

f_{1}=\left[\begin{smallmatrix}p^{m_{r}-(q+1)}&p^{n_{l_{1}}-l_{1}}&\ldots&p^{n_{l_{t}}-l_{t}}\end{smallmatrix}\right]^{T},

f_{3}=\left[\begin{smallmatrix}p^{m_{r}-r}&p^{m_{i_{2}}-i_{2}}&\ldots&p^{m_{i_{s}}-i_{s}}\end{smallmatrix}\right]^{T},

f_{2}=\left[\begin{smallmatrix}p^{m_{i_{1}}-i_{1}}&p^{m_{i_{2}}-i_{2}}&\ldots&p^{m_{i_{s}}-i_{s}}&0&\ldots&0&0\\
0&p^{m_{i_{2}}-i_{2}+r-q-1}&\ldots&p^{m_{i_{s}}-i_{s}+r-q-1}&p^{n_{l_{1}}-l_{1}}&\ldots&p^{n_{l_{t}}-l_{t}}&0\end{smallmatrix}\right]^{T}

and

\Psi=\left[\begin{array}[]{c | c}-1&\begin{array}[]{c c c}0&\ldots&0\end{array}\\
\hline\cr 0&\begin{array}[]{c c c}0&\ldots&0\end{array}\\
\vdots&\begin{array}[]{c c c}\vdots&&\vdots\end{array}\\
0&\begin{array}[]{c c c}0&\ldots&0\end{array}\\
\hline\cr 0&\\
\vdots&Id_{N_{n}}\\
0&\\
\hline\cr 1&\begin{array}[]{c c c c}-p^{c}&0&\ldots&0\end{array}\end{array}\right],\;\Phi=\left[\begin{array}[]{c | c | c |c}\begin{array}[]{c}1\\
\hline\cr 0\\
\vdots\\
0\end{array}&\begin{array}[]{ c}0\ldots 0\\
\hline\cr\\
Id_{N_{m^{\prime\prime}}}\\
\\
\end{array}&\begin{array}[]{c c c}p^{c}&0\ldots&0\\
\hline\cr 0&\ldots&0\\
\vdots&&\vdots\\
0&\ldots&0\end{array}&\begin{array}[]{c}1\\
\hline\cr 0\\
\vdots\\
0\end{array}\end{array}\right],

for
c=m_{i_{1}}-n_{\ell_{1}}-1=m_{r}-n_{q}-1
and
m^{\prime\prime}=(m_{0},\ldots,m_{i_{2}})
.

\square

Lemma 6.3
.

Let
r>q
be the natural numbers and let
m=(m_{0},\ldots,m_{r})
and
n=(n_{0},\ldots,n_{q})
be two strictly increasing sequences with gaps exactly after
m_{i_{1}}>\ldots>m_{i_{s}}
and
n_{l_{1}}>\ldots>n_{l_{t}}
respectively. If
i_{2}=r-1
,
m_{r}=n_{q}+1
, then there exists an exact sequence:

0\to P(n^{\prime})\oplus E_{(m_{r})}\to D(m,n)\to P(m^{\prime})\to 0,

where
n^{\prime}=(n_{0},n_{1},\ldots,n_{q},m_{r})
and
m^{\prime}=(m_{0},\ldots,m_{r-1})
.

Proof.

Note that the sequences
m^{\prime}
,
n^{\prime}
have gaps exactly after
m_{i_{2}}>\ldots>m_{i_{s}}
and
m_{r}>n_{l_{2}}>\ldots>n_{l_{t}}
respectively. Under assumptions of lemma we have

P(n^{\prime})=((p^{m_{r}-(q+1)}\cdot b_{m_{r}+1}+\sum_{j=2}^{t}p^{\ell^{\prime}_{j}}\cdot b^{\prime}_{n_{l_{j}}+1})\subset N_{(m_{r}+1,n_{l_{2}}+1,\ldots,n_{l_{t}}+1)})\oplus E_{(m_{r})},

D(n,m)=((\sum_{j=1}^{s}p^{\ell_{j}}\cdot b_{\beta_{j}},\sum_{j=2}^{s}p^{\ell_{j}+r-q-1}\cdot b_{\beta_{j}}+\sum_{j=1}^{t}p^{\ell^{\prime}_{j}}\cdot b^{\prime}_{\lambda_{j}})\subset\\
\subset N_{(m_{i_{1}}+1,m_{i_{2}}+1,\ldots,m_{i_{s}}+1)}\oplus N_{(n_{l_{1}}+1,n_{l_{2}}+1,\ldots,n_{l_{t}}+1)})

and

P(m^{\prime})=((\sum_{j=2}^{s}p^{\ell_{j}}\cdot b_{m_{i_{j}}+1})\subset N_{(m_{i_{2}}+1,\ldots,m_{i_{s}}+1)}),

where
\ell_{j}=m_{i_{j}}-i_{j}
and
\ell^{\prime}_{j^{\prime}}=n_{l_{j^{\prime}}}-l_{j^{\prime}}
for
1\leq j\leq s
and
1\leq j^{\prime}\leq t.

Since
i_{1}=r
and
l_{1}=q
, we have

m_{r}-(q+1)=n_{q}+1-(q+1)=n_{q}-q=n_{l_{1}}-l_{1}.

Applying this equality, it is straightforward
to check that the following sequence is exact.

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{N_{(n^{\prime})}\oplus E_{(m_{r})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Psi}
\textstyle{N_{(m)}\oplus N_{(n)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Phi}
\textstyle{N_{(m^{\prime})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{N_{(q+2)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\left[\begin{smallmatrix}-p^{r-q-1}\\
1\end{smallmatrix}\right]}
\textstyle{N_{(r+1,q+1)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\left[\begin{smallmatrix}1&p^{r-q-1}\end{smallmatrix}\right]}
\textstyle{N_{(r)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{3}}
\textstyle{0,}

where

f_{1}=\left[\begin{smallmatrix}p^{m_{r}-(q+1)}&p^{n_{l_{2}}-l_{2}}&\ldots&p^{n_{l_{t}}-l_{t}}&0\end{smallmatrix}\right]^{T},

f_{3}=\left[\begin{smallmatrix}p^{m_{i_{2}}-i_{2}}&\ldots&p^{m_{i_{s}}-i_{s}}&\end{smallmatrix}\right]^{T},

f_{2}=\left[\begin{smallmatrix}p^{m_{i_{1}}-i_{1}}&p^{m_{i_{2}}-i_{2}}&\ldots&p^{m_{i_{s}}-i_{s}}&0&\ldots&0\\
0&p^{m_{i_{2}}-i_{2}+r-q-1}&\ldots&p^{m_{i_{s}}-i_{s}+r-q-1}&p^{n_{l_{1}}-l_{1}}&\ldots&p^{n_{l_{t}}-l_{t}}\end{smallmatrix}\right]^{T}

and

\Psi=\left[\begin{array}[]{c | c | c}-1&\begin{array}[]{c c c}0&\ldots&0\end{array}&0\\
\hline\cr 0&\begin{array}[]{c c c}0&\ldots&0\end{array}&0\\
\vdots&\begin{array}[]{c c c}\vdots&&\vdots\end{array}&\vdots\\
0&\begin{array}[]{c c c}0&\ldots&0\end{array}&0\\
1&\begin{array}[]{c c c}0&\ldots&0\end{array}&1\\
\hline\cr 0&Id_{N_{n^{\prime\prime}}}&0\end{array}\right],\quad\quad\Phi=\left[\begin{array}[]{c | c | c }\begin{array}[]{c}0\\
\vdots\\
0\end{array}&\begin{array}[]{c}\\
Id_{N_{m^{\prime}}}\\
\end{array}&\begin{array}[]{c c c}0&\ldots&0\\
\vdots&&\vdots\\
0&\ldots&0\end{array}\end{array}\right]

where
(n^{\prime\prime})=(n_{0},n_{1},\ldots,n_{l_{2}})
.

\square

Remark:

The Lemmata
6.1
and
6.2
are still true if
n
is an empty sequence. In this situation we assume that
q=-1
and
D(m,())\simeq P(m)
.

We illustrate lemmata by the following example.

\Delta,\Gamma
be the following LR-tableaux

Note that
\Delta\leq_{\rm box}\Gamma
. Indeed
\Delta
is obtained from
\Gamma
by two decreasing box move: the entry in the third column of
\Gamma
can be increased first, next it is possible to increase the entry in the first column and we get
\Delta
. We show that
\Delta\leq_{\rm ext}\Gamma
.

Denote by
\widehat{\Gamma}
the tableau obtained from
\Gamma
by increasing by two the entry in the third column. We have

\Delta\prec_{\rm box}\widehat{\Gamma}\prec_{\rm box}\Gamma
.

We set

\Gamma^{\{1,2,4,7\}}=\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\vtop{\halign{&\opttoksa@YT={\font@YT}\getcolor@YT{\save@YT{\opttoksb@YT}}\nil@YT\getcolor@YT{\startbox@@YT\the\opttoksa@YT\the\opttoksb@YT}#\endbox@YT\cr\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to8.4pt{\vss\hbox to8.00006pt{\hss$\scriptstyle$\hss}\vss}\kern-8.4pt\vrule height=8.4pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 8.00006pt\vrule height=8.4pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-8.4pt\hrule width=8.79993pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 8.00006pt\hrule width=8.79993pt,height=0.39993pt}&\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern then
\Gamma=\Gamma^{\{1,2,4,7\}}\cup\Gamma^{\{3\}}\cup\Gamma^{\{5,6\}}
and
\widehat{\Gamma}=\Gamma^{\{1,2,4,7\}}\cup\widehat{\Gamma}^{\{3,5,6\}}.
Moreover
\Gamma^{\{1,2,4,7\}}
is the tableau of
X=P(0)\oplus P(3,5,6)\oplus E_{(6)}
,
\Gamma^{\{3\}}
is the tableau of
P(4)
,
\Gamma^{\{5,6\}}
is the tableau of
P(2,1)\oplus E_{(2)}
and
\widehat{\Gamma}^{\{3,5,6\}}
is the tableau of
D((1,2,4),())\oplus E_{(2)}
. By lemma
6.1
there exists the following short exact sequence:

0\to P(4)\to D((1,2,4),())\to P(2)\to 0,

so we have also the short exact sequence:

0\to P(4)\oplus X\to D((1,2,4),())\oplus X\oplus E_{(2)}\to P(2)\oplus E_{(2)}\to 0.

By the short exact sequence above we have
\widehat{\Gamma}\leq_{\rm ext}\Gamma
.
Similarly we show that
\Delta\leq_{\rm ext}\widehat{\Gamma}
. For tableaux:

0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to8.4pt{\vss\hbox to8.00006pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-8.4pt\vrule width=0.0pt,height=8.4pt\kern 0.39993pt\kern 8.00006pt\vrule width=0.0pt,height=8.4pt\kern 0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-8.4pt\kern 0.39993pt\kern 8.00006pt\kern 0.39993pt}\nullfont\cr\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to8.4pt{\vss\hbox to8.00006pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-8.4pt\vrule width=0.0pt,height=8.4pt\kern 0.39993pt\kern 8.00006pt\vrule width=0.0pt,height=8.4pt\kern 0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-8.4pt\kern width=0.0pt,height=8.4pt\kern 0.39993pt\kern 8.00006pt\vrule width=0.0pt,height=8.4pt\kern 0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-8.4pt\kern 0.39993pt\kern 8.00006pt\kern 0.39993pt}\nullfont\cr\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to8.4pt{\vss\hbox to8.00006pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-8.4pt\vrule width=0.0pt,height=8.4pt\kern 0.39993pt\kern 8.00006pt\vrule width=0.0pt,height=8.4pt\kern 0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-8.4pt\kern 0.39993pt\kern 8.00006pt\kern 0.39993pt}\nullfont&\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to8.4pt{\vss\hbox to8.00006pt{\hss$\scriptstyle 1$\hss}\vss}\kern-8.4pt\vrule height=8.4pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 8.00006pt\vrule height=8.4pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-8.4pt\hrule width=8.79993pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 8.00006pt\hrule width=8.79993pt,height=0.39993pt}\cr\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to8.4pt{\vss\hbox to8.00006pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-8.4pt\vrule width=0.0pt,height=8.4pt\kern 0.39993pt\kern 8.00006pt\vrule width=0.0pt,height=8.4pt\kern 0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-8.4pt\kern 0.39993pt\kern height=8.4pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 8.00006pt\vrule height=8.4pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-8.4pt\hrule width=8.79993pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 8.00006pt\hrule width=8.79993pt,height=0.39993pt}\cr}}\kern 690.4pt}\kern 690.4pt}}$}

we have
\widehat{\Gamma}=\widehat{\Gamma}^{\{7\}}\cup\widehat{\Gamma}^{\{1,2,4\}}\cup\widehat{\Gamma}^{\{3,5,6\}}
and
\Delta=\widehat{\Gamma}^{\{7\}}\cup\Delta^{\{1,3,5,6\}}\cup\Delta^{\{2,4\}}.
These tableaux can be obtained as a LR-tableaux for the following embeddings:

\widehat{\Gamma}^{\{7\}}
–
P(0)
,
\widehat{\Gamma}^{\{1,2,4\}}
–
P(3,5,6)\oplus E_{(6)}
,
\widehat{\Gamma}^{\{3,5,6\}}
–
P(1,2,4)\oplus E_{(2)}
and
\Delta^{\{1,3,5,6\}}\cup\Delta^{\{2,4\}}

D((1,2,4,6),(3,5))\oplus E_{(2)}
. By lemma
6.3
we have the following short exact sequence:

0\to P(3,5,6)\oplus E_{(6)}\to D((1,2,4,6),(3,5))\to P(1,2,4)\to 0.

The sequence:

0\to P(3,5,6)\oplus E_{(6)}\oplus Y\to D((1,2,4,6),(3,5))\oplus Y\to P(1,2,4)\to 0

is also exact, for
Y=E_{(2)}\oplus P(0)
, so
\Delta\leq_{\rm ext}\widehat{\Gamma}
and finally
\Delta\leq_{\rm ext}\Gamma.

Proposition 6.4
.

Suppose
\Delta,\Gamma
are LR-tableaux of the same shape, both are rook strips and let
\Delta
be obtained from
\Gamma
by increasing an entry, then
\Delta\leq_{\rm ext}\Gamma
.

Proof.

Let
\Delta,\Gamma
be LR-tableaux of the same shape and let both be rook strips. Suppose that
\Delta,\Gamma
differ by an entry in the column number
i
. Then the tableau
\Delta^{\prime}
obtained from
\Delta
by deleting the
i
-th column is an LR-tableau. Observe that
\Delta^{\prime}
can be obtain also from
\Gamma
by deleting the column
i
. Let
m
(resp.
m^{\prime}
)
be the entry of
\Delta
(resp.
\Gamma
) in
i
-th column. Since
\Delta^{\prime}
is the LR-tableau obtained from
\Delta
by deleting the
i
-th column with entry
m
, we can choose indices
i=i_{m}<i_{m-1}<\ldots<i_{1}
, such that in the column of number
i_{t}
of
\Delta^{\prime}
we have the entry
t
and the tableau
\Delta^{\prime\prime}
obtained from
\Delta
by deleting columns
i_{m},i_{m-1},\ldots,i_{1}
is an LR-tableau. Moreover
\Gamma^{\prime}=\Delta^{\prime\prime}\cup\Gamma^{\{i\}}
is also an LR-tableaux, where

\Gamma^{\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{k}\}}
denotes diagram created from columns
i_{1},\ldots,i_{k}
of
\Gamma
. Similarly as above we can choose indices
i=j_{m^{\prime}}<j_{m^{\prime}-1}<\ldots<j_{1}
, such that in the column
j_{t}
of
\Gamma^{\prime}
we have the entry
t
and the tableau
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}
obtained from
\Gamma^{\prime}
by deleting columns
j_{m^{\prime}},j_{m^{\prime}-1},\ldots,j_{1}
is an LR-tableau (for simplifying notation, we use the indexes from the tableau
\Gamma
instead
\Gamma^{\prime}
). The LR-tableaux
\Delta,\Gamma
can be decompose into:

\Delta=\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\cup\Delta^{\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{m},j_{1},\ldots,j_{m^{\prime}-1}\}}\quad{\rm and}\quad\Gamma=\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\cup\Gamma^{\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{m-1}\}}\cup\Gamma^{\{j_{1},\ldots,j_{m^{\prime}}\}}.

Note that by Lemma
4.2
and Proposition
4.3
, the tableau
\Gamma^{\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{m-1}\}}
(resp.
\Gamma^{\{j_{1},\ldots,j_{m^{\prime}}\}}
) is the LR-tableau of the embedding
P(i_{1}^{\prime}-1,\ldots,i_{m-1}^{\prime}-1)\oplus E_{\beta}
(resp.
P(j_{1}^{\prime}-1,\ldots,j_{m^{\prime}}^{\prime}-1)\oplus E_{\beta^{\prime}}
),
where by
i^{\prime}_{t}
(resp.
j^{\prime}_{t}
) we denote the length of
i_{t}
-th (resp.
j_{t}
-th) column of
\Gamma
and
\beta
(resp.
\beta^{\prime}
) is the partition with entries
i^{\prime}_{t}
(resp.
j^{\prime}_{t}
) satisfying
i^{\prime}_{t}=i^{\prime}_{t+1}+1
(resp.
j^{\prime}_{t}=j^{\prime}_{t+1}+1
). Similarly, applying also Lemma
4.4
, we get that
\Delta^{\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{m},j_{1},\ldots,j_{m-1}\}}
is the LR-tableau of
D((i_{1}^{\prime}-1,\ldots,i_{m}^{\prime}-1),(j_{1}^{\prime}-1,\ldots,j_{m^{\prime}-1}^{\prime}-1))\oplus E_{\beta}\oplus E_{\beta^{\prime}}
.

If
i_{m}^{\prime}>i_{m-1}^{\prime}+1
and
i_{m}^{\prime}>j_{m^{\prime}}^{\prime}+1
, then by Lemma
6.1
the following sequence:

0\to P(j_{1}^{\prime}-1,\ldots,j_{m^{\prime}}^{\prime}-1)\to D((i_{1}^{\prime}-1,\ldots,i_{m}^{\prime}-1),(j_{1}^{\prime}-1,\ldots,j_{m^{\prime}-1}^{\prime}-1))\to\\
\to P(i_{1}^{\prime}-1,\ldots,i_{m-1}^{\prime}-1)\to 0

is exact and we are done.

In the remaining cases short exact sequences are obtained by Lemma
6.2
if
i_{m}^{\prime}=i_{m-1}^{\prime}+1
and
i_{m}^{\prime}>j_{m^{\prime}}^{\prime}+1
, and by Lemma
6.3
if
i_{m}^{\prime}>i_{m-1}^{\prime}+1
and
i_{m}^{\prime}>j_{m^{\prime}}^{\prime}+1
.

If
i_{m}^{\prime}=i_{m-1}^{\prime}+1
and
i_{m}^{\prime}=j_{m^{\prime}}^{\prime}+1
, then
i_{m-1}^{\prime}=j_{m^{\prime}}^{\prime}
, so
\Delta,\Gamma
are not rook strips, and the lemma follows.

\square

Lemma 6.5
.

Suppose
\Delta,\Gamma
are LR-tableaux of the same shape
\beta\setminus\gamma
that is a rook strip. If
\Delta\leq_{\rm box}\Gamma
, then
\Delta\leq_{\rm ext}\Gamma

Proof.

Let
\Delta,\Gamma
be LR-tableaux of the same shape
\beta\setminus\gamma
that is a rook strip.
If
\Delta\leq_{\rm box}\Gamma
, then by definition there exist a sequence of LR-tableaux such that

\Delta=\Delta^{(0)}\prec_{\rm box}\Delta^{(1)}\prec_{\rm box}\ldots\prec_{\rm box}\Delta^{(s)}=\Gamma.

Fix
i=0,1\ldots s-1
.

If
\Delta^{(i)}
is obtained from
\Delta^{(i+1)}
by swapping two entries, such that the
smaller entry winds up in the lower position, then we obtain
\Delta^{(i)}\leq_{\rm ext}\Delta^{(i+1)}
, by
[
13
, Section 4.]
.
If
\Delta^{(i)}
is obtained from
\Delta^{(i+1)}
by increasing an entry we have
\Delta^{(i)}\leq_{\rm ext}\Delta^{(i+1)}
by Proposition
6.4
.
So

\Delta=\Delta^{(0)}\leq_{\rm ext}\Delta^{(1)}\leq_{\rm ext}\ldots\leq_{\rm ext}\Delta^{(s)}=\Gamma

and by definition of
\leq_{\rm ext}
we have
\Delta\leq_{\rm ext}\Gamma.

\square

Proof of Theorem
1.1
Fix an integer
r\geq 1
. By Lemmata
6.5
and
5.1
, Proposition
5.2
we have
\leq_{\rm box}\subseteq\leq_{\rm ext}\subseteq\leq_{\rm hom}\subseteq\leq_{\rm dom}
on

\mathcal{T}_{r,\gamma}^{\beta}
for arbitrary partitions
\beta,\gamma
such that
\beta\setminus\gamma
is a rook strip and
|\beta|-|\gamma|=r
. By Lemma
3.2
we have
\leq_{\rm dom}\subseteq\leq_{\rm box}
on
\mathcal{T}_{r}
. Lemma
2.5
finishes the proof.

As a consequence we get also.

Theorem 6.6
.

For any integer
r\geq 1
, for arbitrary partitions
\beta,\gamma
such that
\beta\setminus\gamma
is a rook strip and
|\beta|-|\gamma|=r
, we have

\leq_{\rm box}=\leq_{\rm ext}=\leq_{\rm hom}\leq_{\rm dom}

on
\mathcal{T}_{r,\gamma}^{\beta}
.
